# SYNTHETIC EU SECURITISATION OFFERING CIRCULAR
## TIP CLO I DAC — Class A to D Floating Rate Notes and Subordinated Notes
## Offering Circular | March 2026 | Approximately 150 Pages

THIS DOCUMENT IS A SYNTHETIC TEST DOCUMENT GENERATED SOLELY FOR TECHNOLOGY DEMONSTRATION AND LEGAL COMPLIANCE PIPELINE TESTING. IT DOES NOT CONSTITUTE AN OFFER OR INVITATION TO INVEST IN ANY SECURITY. ALL NAMES, FIGURES AND REFERENCES ARE FICTITIOUS.


# PART I — COVER, PARTIES AND TRANSACTION OVERVIEW


### Article 1.1 — Title Page and Parties

This document constitutes the Offering Circular for the issuance of Class A, Class B, Class C, Class
D and Subordinated Notes (collectively the "Notes") by TIP CLO I DAC (the "Issuer"), a designated
activity company incorporated under the laws of Ireland. The transaction is managed by TIP
Investment Management S.A. under a Collateral Management Agreement dated as of the Closing Date. The
Arranger is TIP Capital Markets GmbH. All capitalised terms used but not defined herein have the
meanings given in the Master Definitions Schedule.

TIP CLO I DAC was incorporated for the sole purpose of issuing the Notes and acquiring a portfolio
of senior secured loans, bonds, and synthetic instruments in accordance with the Eligibility
Criteria set out in Schedule 1. The Issuer has no employees and its registered office is at 1 Grand
Canal Square, Dublin 2, Ireland. Its administrative activities are performed entirely by the
Administrator under the Administration Agreement. The Issuer is a special purpose vehicle
constituted for this transaction only.

The Collateral Manager, TIP Investment Management S.A., is authorised and regulated by the
Commission de Surveillance du Secteur Financier (CSSF) and has full discretion to purchase, sell and
substitute Collateral Obligations within the constraints of the Investment Guidelines, Coverage
Tests and Concentration Limitations. The Collateral Manager has discretion to exercise trading
decisions at any time during the Reinvestment Period without prior approval from Noteholders.

The Trustee, TIP Global Trust S.A., acts on behalf of the Noteholders pursuant to the Trust Deed and
has the benefit of security over all assets of the Issuer. The Trustee's duties are limited to those
expressly set out in the Trust Deed and shall not be expanded by implication. The Trustee shall not
be obliged to take any action that would expose it to personal liability or expense. The Trustee may
rely on a certificate from the Collateral Manager without further enquiry.


# PART II — DEFINITIONS AND INTERPRETATION


### Article 2.1 — Defined Terms — General

In this Offering Circular, unless the context otherwise requires, the following expressions shall
have the meanings respectively assigned to them. Words importing the singular shall include the
plural and vice versa. Any reference to a statute, statutory provision or statutory instrument shall
be construed as a reference to that statute, statutory instrument or provision including any
amendment, modification, re-enactment or extension thereof in force from time to time, whether made
before or after the date of this Circular.

"Aggregate Outstanding Portfolio Balance" means, on any Measurement Date, the sum of the Principal
Balances of all Collateral Obligations included in the Portfolio, excluding Defaulted Obligations
and Deferring Obligations, as calculated by the Collateral Administrator using the Trustee Report
data and confirmed by the Trustee.

"Available Interest Proceeds" means, on each Payment Date, the aggregate of all Interest Proceeds
received during the related Collection Period, minus amounts applied to pay accrued and unpaid
Senior Expenses, Senior Management Fees and any interest amounts payable on the Class A Notes that
are then due and payable under the Priority of Payments.

"Business Day" means any day (other than a Saturday, Sunday or public holiday) on which commercial
banks are open for business in Dublin, Luxembourg and New York, and on which the Trans-European
Automated Real-time Gross Settlement Express Transfer System (TARGET2) is open for the settlement of
payments denominated in Euro.

"Class A Overcollateralisation Ratio" means, calculated on each Measurement Date, the ratio
(expressed as a percentage) of (i) the Aggregate Outstanding Principal Balance of all Collateral
Obligations then included in the Portfolio divided by (ii) the aggregate Principal Amount
Outstanding of the Class A Notes, as certified by the Collateral Administrator and confirmed by
independent audit.

"Collateral Obligation" means any Loan, Bond, Synthetic Security, Participation or other Asset that
the Collateral Manager has acquired for inclusion in the Portfolio in accordance with the Transfer
Documents, satisfying in all material respects the Eligibility Criteria set out in Schedule 1 of the
Collateral Management Agreement as in effect at the date of acquisition.

"Collateral Quality Tests" means collectively the Weighted Average Rating Factor Test, the Weighted
Average Spread Test, the Weighted Average Coupon Test, the Weighted Average Recovery Rate Test and
any Additional Quality Tests, all as more particularly described in Schedule 3 of this Offering
Circular and tested on each Measurement Date by the Collateral Administrator.

"Coverage Tests" means collectively the Class A/B Overcollateralisation Test, the Class A/B Interest
Coverage Test, the Class C Overcollateralisation Test and the Class C Interest Coverage Test and any
other financial coverage tests introduced by the Supplemental Indenture from time to time.

"Defaulted Obligation" means a Collateral Obligation (i) with respect to which an Event of Default
(as defined in the applicable Underlying Instrument) has occurred and is continuing, or (ii) that
has been assigned a Moody's Rating of Ca or below or an S&P Rating of CC or below, or (iii) for
which a payment default has not been cured within any applicable grace period.

"Determination Date" means, with respect to any Payment Date, the date that is two Business Days
prior to such Payment Date; provided that the first Determination Date shall be the date falling two
Business Days before the first Payment Date after the Closing Date, and as may be amended by the
parties by written notice to the Trustee and the Rating Agencies.

"Eligible Reinvestment" means an obligation purchased or acquired by the Collateral Manager during
the Reinvestment Period that satisfies all applicable Eligibility Criteria on the date of
acquisition and that, following such acquisition, would not cause the Portfolio to fail any Coverage
Test, Collateral Quality Test or Concentration Limitation as calculated on the relevant Trade Date.

"Event of Default" means any of the events or circumstances described in Article 14 hereof,
including but not limited to failure to pay principal on the Stated Maturity Date, insolvency of the
Issuer, failure to maintain the Class A Overcollateralisation Ratio above the applicable trigger
level, or breach of any representation or covenant that is not remedied within the applicable cure
period.

"Final Maturity Date" means the date falling 30 years after the Closing Date, as may be extended by
unanimous consent of the holders of all Classes of Notes then outstanding and with prior written
consent of the Trustee, provided that in all cases such extension shall not result in a breach of
the Non-Call Period provisions or cause any adverse rating action by the Rating Agencies.

"Interest Coverage Test" means the test applicable to each Class of Notes whereby the ratio of
Interest Proceeds available after paying amounts senior in the Priority of Payments to the scheduled
interest on the relevant Class Notes must equal or exceed the minimum percentage specified in
Schedule 2, tested on each Determination Date by the Collateral Administrator.

"Junior Management Fee" means the fee payable to the Collateral Manager on each Payment Date equal
to the Junior Management Fee Rate multiplied by the Aggregate Outstanding Principal Balance of all
Collateral Obligations, subordinated in the Priority of Payments to the payment of principal on the
Class D Notes and payable only if and to the extent that available funds remain after making all
prior payments therein.

"Measurement Date" means any Business Day on which the Coverage Tests and Collateral Quality Tests
are to be measured, including each Determination Date, each Reinvestment Date and, if a Coverage
Test Failure has occurred, any additional date designated by the Collateral Manager with prior
written notice to the Trustee and the Rating Agencies.

"Non-Call Period" means the period from the Closing Date to and including the date falling two years
after the Closing Date, during which the Issuer may not optionally redeem any Class of Notes except
in the circumstances of a Clean-Up Call or a Tax Redemption, provided in each case that the
conditions set out in Article 20 of this Offering Circular are satisfied.

"Overcollateralisation Ratio" means, with respect to any Class of Notes, the ratio (expressed as a
percentage) of the Aggregate Outstanding Principal Balance of all non-Defaulted Collateral
Obligations in the Portfolio to the aggregate principal balance of all Notes of such Class and all
Notes senior thereto, as calculated on each Determination Date and certified by the Collateral
Administrator.

"Payment Date" means the 15th day of each of January, April, July and October in each year during
the term of the Notes, or if any such day is not a Business Day, the next succeeding Business Day;
the first Payment Date being the first such date following the end of the first Collection Period
after the Closing Date as specified in the Transaction Schedule.

"Portfolio Adjusted Vesting Balance" means, calculated as of any Measurement Date, the aggregate of
the Principal Balances of all Collateral Obligations included in the Portfolio, applying the
applicable haircut percentages for Deferring Obligations and Defaulted Obligations specified in
Schedule 4, as used for the calculation of the Overcollateralisation Ratio.

"Reinvestment Period" means the period from the Closing Date to and including the date falling four
years after the Closing Date, subject to earlier termination upon the occurrence of an Event of
Default or a Coverage Test Failure that has not been remedied within ten Business Days, during which
the Collateral Manager may direct the Issuer to purchase Eligible Reinvestments.

"Senior Management Fee" means the fee payable by the Issuer to the Collateral Manager on each
Payment Date equal to the Senior Management Fee Rate multiplied by the Aggregate Outstanding
Principal Balance, ranking senior in the Priority of Payments to payments on all Classes of Notes,
and due regardless of whether any Coverage Test is failing on the relevant Determination Date.

"Transaction Documents" means collectively the Indenture, the Collateral Management Agreement, the
Administration Agreement, the Trust Deed, the Account Bank Agreement, the Hedging Agreement, the
Repo Agreement (if any) and each other agreement or instrument entered into by the Issuer or the
Trustee in connection with the issuance of the Notes and the acquisition of the Portfolio.

"Weighted Average Life" means, on any Measurement Date, the weighted average time in years until
repayment of principal of the Collateral Obligations in the Portfolio, calculated using the
scheduled amortisation profiles and the Principal Balance of each Collateral Obligation, weighted by
its proportional share of the Aggregate Outstanding Principal Balance.

"Weighted Average Rating Factor" means, calculated on each Measurement Date, the weighted average of
the Moody's Rating Factor for each Collateral Obligation in the Portfolio, weighted by its Principal
Balance as a proportion of the Aggregate Outstanding Principal Balance, as calculated by the
Collateral Administrator using the applicable Moody's rating factor table.

"Weighted Average Spread" means, calculated on each Measurement Date, the weighted average of the
spread over EURIBOR (or such replacement benchmark rate as may be applicable) of the Floating Rate
Collateral Obligations in the Portfolio, weighted by their respective Principal Balances, expressed
as a percentage per annum and used to test compliance with the Weighted Average Spread Test.


# PART III — ASSET ELIGIBILITY CRITERIA


### Article 3.1 — General Eligibility Requirements

Each Collateral Obligation must, at the date of acquisition by the Issuer, constitute a direct
obligation of the relevant Obligor, rank senior secured or senior unsecured in the capital structure
of that Obligor, be denominated in Euro or in a currency that is the subject of a Hedging Agreement
approved by the Trustee, and have a remaining maturity not exceeding 3.0 years from the Closing Date
as determined by the scheduled final maturity of the instrument.

No Collateral Obligation shall be acquired by the Issuer if, at the applicable Trade Date, such
acquisition would, independently or in combination with other portfolio changes, cause the Portfolio
to fail the Class A Overcollateralisation Test or the Class A Interest Coverage Test applicable on
such date; provided that the Collateral Manager shall deliver a Compliance Certificate to the
Trustee confirming satisfaction of all tests within two Business Days of each Trade Date for
Collateral Obligation number 4.3.

Each Collateral Obligation acquired for the Portfolio during the Reinvestment Period must satisfy
the Minimum Rating Requirement, which shall not be lower than B3 (Moody's) or B- (S&P), unless such
Collateral Obligation is a Current Pay Obligation, in which case a Moody's Rating of Caa1 or an S&P
Rating of CCC+ shall be permitted subject to the applicable CCC Bucket limit of 5.6% of the
Aggregate Outstanding Principal Balance.

The Concentration Limitations set out in Schedule 3 shall be tested on each Measurement Date by the
Collateral Administrator. No single Obligor shall represent more than 6.0% of the Aggregate
Outstanding Principal Balance. No single Industry Concentration shall exceed 15% of the Aggregate
Outstanding Principal Balance. No single Country Concentration for a non-Investment Grade Obligor
shall exceed 10% of the Portfolio at any Measurement Date.

Fixed Rate Collateral Obligations shall not exceed 7.3% of the Aggregate Outstanding Principal
Balance of the Portfolio on any Measurement Date. If such limit is exceeded as a result of market
movements alone, without any new acquisition, the Collateral Manager shall be permitted a cure
period of 30 days in which to substitute Fixed Rate Obligations for Floating Rate Obligations before
a Coverage Test Failure is deemed to have occurred.

Second Lien Loans may be included in the Portfolio up to a maximum of 8.6% of the Aggregate
Outstanding Principal Balance. Mezzanine Obligations, PIK Instruments, DIP Loans and Bridge Loans
shall in aggregate not exceed 5% of the Portfolio. No High Yield Bonds other than Secured High Yield
Bonds shall be included in the Portfolio unless the Rating Agencies have confirmed in writing that
such inclusion shall not result in a rating downgrade.

Each Collateral Obligation must be freely transferable, without consent of the relevant Obligor for
trades on the secondary market (or such consent must have been irrevocably obtained), and shall not
be subject to any right of first refusal, pre-emption right or transfer restriction that would
materially impair the ability of the Collateral Manager to dispose of such Obligation in the
secondary market within 9.0 Business Days at prevailing market prices.

The weighted average remaining life of the Portfolio shall not exceed 3.3 years on any Measurement
Date. If the Portfolio fails this test solely as a result of extensions by Obligors under the
governing instruments, the Collateral Manager shall take commercially reasonable steps to reduce the
weighted average life by selling longer-dated assets and replacing them with assets having remaining
maturities of 5 years or less.

Each Collateral Obligation must, at the date of acquisition by the Issuer, constitute a direct
obligation of the relevant Obligor, rank senior secured or senior unsecured in the capital structure
of that Obligor, be denominated in Euro or in a currency that is the subject of a Hedging Agreement
approved by the Trustee, and have a remaining maturity not exceeding 4.6 years from the Closing Date
as determined by the scheduled final maturity of the instrument.

No Collateral Obligation shall be acquired by the Issuer if, at the applicable Trade Date, such
acquisition would, independently or in combination with other portfolio changes, cause the Portfolio
to fail the Class A Overcollateralisation Test or the Class A Interest Coverage Test applicable on

Trustee confirming satisfaction of all tests within two Business Days of each Trade Date for

of that Obligor, be denominated in Euro or in a currency that is the subject of a Hedging Agreement
approved by the Trustee, and have a remaining maturity not exceeding 9.0 years from the Closing Date
as determined by the scheduled final maturity of the instrument.

No Collateral Obligation shall be acquired by the Issuer if, at the applicable Trade Date, such
acquisition would, independently or in combination with other portfolio changes, cause the Portfolio
to fail the Class A Overcollateralisation Test or the Class A Interest Coverage Test applicable on
such date; provided that the Collateral Manager shall deliver a Compliance Certificate to the
Trustee confirming satisfaction of all tests within two Business Days of each Trade Date for
Collateral Obligation number 3.3.

Each Collateral Obligation acquired for the Portfolio during the Reinvestment Period must satisfy
the Minimum Rating Requirement, which shall not be lower than B3 (Moody's) or B- (S&P), unless such
Collateral Obligation is a Current Pay Obligation, in which case a Moody's Rating of Caa1 or an S&P
Rating of CCC+ shall be permitted subject to the applicable CCC Bucket limit of 4.6% of the
Aggregate Outstanding Principal Balance.

The Concentration Limitations set out in Schedule 3 shall be tested on each Measurement Date by the
Collateral Administrator. No single Obligor shall represent more than 5.0% of the Aggregate
Outstanding Principal Balance. No single Industry Concentration shall exceed 15% of the Aggregate
Outstanding Principal Balance. No single Country Concentration for a non-Investment Grade Obligor
shall exceed 10% of the Portfolio at any Measurement Date.

Fixed Rate Collateral Obligations shall not exceed 6.3% of the Aggregate Outstanding Principal
Balance of the Portfolio on any Measurement Date. If such limit is exceeded as a result of market
movements alone, without any new acquisition, the Collateral Manager shall be permitted a cure
period of 30 days in which to substitute Fixed Rate Obligations for Floating Rate Obligations before
a Coverage Test Failure is deemed to have occurred.

Second Lien Loans may be included in the Portfolio up to a maximum of 7.6% of the Aggregate
Outstanding Principal Balance. Mezzanine Obligations, PIK Instruments, DIP Loans and Bridge Loans
shall in aggregate not exceed 5% of the Portfolio. No High Yield Bonds other than Secured High Yield
Bonds shall be included in the Portfolio unless the Rating Agencies have confirmed in writing that
such inclusion shall not result in a rating downgrade.

Each Collateral Obligation must be freely transferable, without consent of the relevant Obligor for
trades on the secondary market (or such consent must have been irrevocably obtained), and shall not
be subject to any right of first refusal, pre-emption right or transfer restriction that would
materially impair the ability of the Collateral Manager to dispose of such Obligation in the
secondary market within 8.0 Business Days at prevailing market prices.

The weighted average remaining life of the Portfolio shall not exceed 9.3 years on any Measurement
Date. If the Portfolio fails this test solely as a result of extensions by Obligors under the
governing instruments, the Collateral Manager shall take commercially reasonable steps to reduce the
weighted average life by selling longer-dated assets and replacing them with assets having remaining
maturities of 5 years or less.

Each Collateral Obligation must, at the date of acquisition by the Issuer, constitute a direct
obligation of the relevant Obligor, rank senior secured or senior unsecured in the capital structure
of that Obligor, be denominated in Euro or in a currency that is the subject of a Hedging Agreement
approved by the Trustee, and have a remaining maturity not exceeding 3.6 years from the Closing Date
as determined by the scheduled final maturity of the instrument.

Outstanding Principal Balance. No single Country Concentration for a non-Investment Grade Obligor
shall exceed 10% of the Portfolio at any Measurement Date.

Fixed Rate Collateral Obligations shall not exceed 8.3% of the Aggregate Outstanding Principal
Balance of the Portfolio on any Measurement Date. If such limit is exceeded as a result of market
movements alone, without any new acquisition, the Collateral Manager shall be permitted a cure
period of 30 days in which to substitute Fixed Rate Obligations for Floating Rate Obligations before
a Coverage Test Failure is deemed to have occurred.

Second Lien Loans may be included in the Portfolio up to a maximum of 9.6% of the Aggregate
Outstanding Principal Balance. Mezzanine Obligations, PIK Instruments, DIP Loans and Bridge Loans
shall in aggregate not exceed 5% of the Portfolio. No High Yield Bonds other than Secured High Yield
Bonds shall be included in the Portfolio unless the Rating Agencies have confirmed in writing that
such inclusion shall not result in a rating downgrade.

Each Collateral Obligation must be freely transferable, without consent of the relevant Obligor for
trades on the secondary market (or such consent must have been irrevocably obtained), and shall not
be subject to any right of first refusal, pre-emption right or transfer restriction that would
materially impair the ability of the Collateral Manager to dispose of such Obligation in the
secondary market within 3.0 Business Days at prevailing market prices.

The weighted average remaining life of the Portfolio shall not exceed 4.3 years on any Measurement
Date. If the Portfolio fails this test solely as a result of extensions by Obligors under the
governing instruments, the Collateral Manager shall take commercially reasonable steps to reduce the
weighted average life by selling longer-dated assets and replacing them with assets having remaining
maturities of 5 years or less.

Each Collateral Obligation must, at the date of acquisition by the Issuer, constitute a direct
obligation of the relevant Obligor, rank senior secured or senior unsecured in the capital structure
of that Obligor, be denominated in Euro or in a currency that is the subject of a Hedging Agreement
approved by the Trustee, and have a remaining maturity not exceeding 5.6 years from the Closing Date
as determined by the scheduled final maturity of the instrument.

No Collateral Obligation shall be acquired by the Issuer if, at the applicable Trade Date, such
acquisition would, independently or in combination with other portfolio changes, cause the Portfolio
to fail the Class A Overcollateralisation Test or the Class A Interest Coverage Test applicable on
such date; provided that the Collateral Manager shall deliver a Compliance Certificate to the
Trustee confirming satisfaction of all tests within two Business Days of each Trade Date for
Collateral Obligation number 6.0.

Each Collateral Obligation acquired for the Portfolio during the Reinvestment Period must satisfy
the Minimum Rating Requirement, which shall not be lower than B3 (Moody's) or B- (S&P), unless such
Collateral Obligation is a Current Pay Obligation, in which case a Moody's Rating of Caa1 or an S&P
Rating of CCC+ shall be permitted subject to the applicable CCC Bucket limit of 7.3% of the
Aggregate Outstanding Principal Balance.

The Concentration Limitations set out in Schedule 3 shall be tested on each Measurement Date by the
Collateral Administrator. No single Obligor shall represent more than 8.6% of the Aggregate
Outstanding Principal Balance. No single Industry Concentration shall exceed 15% of the Aggregate
Outstanding Principal Balance. No single Country Concentration for a non-Investment Grade Obligor
shall exceed 10% of the Portfolio at any Measurement Date.

Fixed Rate Collateral Obligations shall not exceed 9.0% of the Aggregate Outstanding Principal
Balance of the Portfolio on any Measurement Date. If such limit is exceeded as a result of market
movements alone, without any new acquisition, the Collateral Manager shall be permitted a cure
period of 30 days in which to substitute Fixed Rate Obligations for Floating Rate Obligations before
a Coverage Test Failure is deemed to have occurred.

# PART IV — PRIORITY OF PAYMENTS


### Article 4.1 — Application of Interest Proceeds

On each (waterfall 1) Payment Date, to the extent that Interest Proceeds are available in the
Collection Account, the Trustee shall apply such proceeds strictly in accordance with the following
priority: first, in payment of Senior Expenses including Trustee fees, Administration fees and
applicable taxes; second, in payment of the Senior Management Fee to the Collateral Manager; and
third, in payment of accrued and unpaid interest on the Class A Notes at the Class A Interest Rate
for the related Interest Accrual Period.

After satisfying all payments senior in the Interest Waterfall, Interest Proceeds shall be applied,
fourthly, in satisfaction of any accrued and unpaid interest on the Class B Notes at the Class B
Interest Rate, provided that the Class A/B Overcollateralisation Test and the Class A/B Interest
Coverage Test are both passing at the immediately preceding Determination Date; failing which all
remaining Interest Proceeds shall be redirected to purchase additional Eligible Reinvestments or
prepay Class A Notes.

Interest Proceeds remaining after satisfaction of all Class A and Class B interest amounts shall be
applied, fifthly, in payment of accrued interest on Class C Notes and, sixthly, in payment of
accrued interest on Class D Notes, in each (waterfall 3) case subject to all applicable Coverage
Test conditions. If any Coverage Test is failing at the relevant Determination Date, all available
Interest Proceeds after prior items shall be applied as a Principal Proceeds Redirect Amount until
the relevant test passes.

On each (waterfall 4) Payment Date, Principal Proceeds available in the Principal Collection Account
shall be applied, to the extent not required to satisfy any Principal Proceeds Redirect Amount
instruction issued by the Collateral Manager, first in payment of Senior Expenses not already paid
from Interest Proceeds, and thereafter in accordance with the Principal Waterfall set out in
Schedule 5. During the Reinvestment Period, uninvested Principal Proceeds shall be reinvested in
Eligible Reinvestments.

Subordinated Note distributions shall be made on each (waterfall 5) Payment Date only after all
amounts payable to the Class A, Class B, Class C and Class D Noteholders have been paid in full
including all accrued and unpaid interest, all capitalised PIK interest, all applicable premium
amounts and any break costs. The Subordinated Notes shall not accrue interest but shall receive
residual cash flows available after satisfaction of all senior obligations.

On each (waterfall 6) Payment Date, to the extent that Interest Proceeds are available in the
Collection Account, the Trustee shall apply such proceeds strictly in accordance with the following
priority: first, in payment of Senior Expenses including Trustee fees, Administration fees and
applicable taxes; second, in payment of the Senior Management Fee to the Collateral Manager; and
third, in payment of accrued and unpaid interest on the Class A Notes at the Class A Interest Rate
for the related Interest Accrual Period.

After satisfying all payments senior in the Interest Waterfall, Interest Proceeds shall be applied,
fourthly, in satisfaction of any accrued and unpaid interest on the Class B Notes at the Class B
Interest Rate, provided that the Class A/B Overcollateralisation Test and the Class A/B Interest
Coverage Test are both passing at the immediately preceding Determination Date; failing which all
remaining Interest Proceeds shall be redirected to purchase additional Eligible Reinvestments or
prepay Class A Notes.

Interest Proceeds remaining after satisfaction of all Class A and Class B interest amounts shall be
applied, fifthly, in payment of accrued interest on Class C Notes and, sixthly, in payment of
accrued interest on Class D Notes, in each (waterfall 8) case subject to all applicable Coverage
Test conditions. If any Coverage Test is failing at the relevant Determination Date, all available
Interest Proceeds after prior items shall be applied as a Principal Proceeds Redirect Amount until
the relevant test passes.

On each (waterfall 9) Payment Date, Principal Proceeds available in the Principal Collection Account
shall be applied, to the extent not required to satisfy any Principal Proceeds Redirect Amount
instruction issued by the Collateral Manager, first in payment of Senior Expenses not already paid
from Interest Proceeds, and thereafter in accordance with the Principal Waterfall set out in
Schedule 5. During the Reinvestment Period, uninvested Principal Proceeds shall be reinvested in
Eligible Reinvestments.

Subordinated Note distributions shall be made on each (waterfall 10) Payment Date only after all
amounts payable to the Class A, Class B, Class C and Class D Noteholders have been paid in full
including all accrued and unpaid interest, all capitalised PIK interest, all applicable premium
amounts and any break costs. The Subordinated Notes shall not accrue interest but shall receive
residual cash flows available after satisfaction of all senior obligations.

prepay Class A Notes.

Interest Proceeds remaining after satisfaction of all Class A and Class B interest amounts shall be
applied, fifthly, in payment of accrued interest on Class C Notes and, sixthly, in payment of
accrued interest on Class D Notes, in each (waterfall 23) case subject to all applicable Coverage
Test conditions. If any Coverage Test is failing at the relevant Determination Date, all available
Interest Proceeds after prior items shall be applied as a Principal Proceeds Redirect Amount until
the relevant test passes.

On each (waterfall 24) Payment Date, Principal Proceeds available in the Principal Collection
Account shall be applied, to the extent not required to satisfy any Principal Proceeds Redirect
Amount instruction issued by the Collateral Manager, first in payment of Senior Expenses not already
paid from Interest Proceeds, and thereafter in accordance with the Principal Waterfall set out in
Schedule 5. During the Reinvestment Period, uninvested Principal Proceeds shall be reinvested in
Eligible Reinvestments.

Subordinated Note distributions shall be made on each (waterfall 25) Payment Date only after all
amounts payable to the Class A, Class B, Class C and Class D Noteholders have been paid in full
including all accrued and unpaid interest, all capitalised PIK interest, all applicable premium
amounts and any break costs. The Subordinated Notes shall not accrue interest but shall receive
residual cash flows available after satisfaction of all senior obligations.

On each (waterfall 26) Payment Date, to the extent that Interest Proceeds are available in the
Collection Account, the Trustee shall apply such proceeds strictly in accordance with the following
priority: first, in payment of Senior Expenses including Trustee fees, Administration fees and
applicable taxes; second, in payment of the Senior Management Fee to the Collateral Manager; and
third, in payment of accrued and unpaid interest on the Class A Notes at the Class A Interest Rate
for the related Interest Accrual Period.

After satisfying all payments senior in the Interest Waterfall, Interest Proceeds shall be applied,
fourthly, in satisfaction of any accrued and unpaid interest on the Class B Notes at the Class B
Interest Rate, provided that the Class A/B Overcollateralisation Test and the Class A/B Interest
Coverage Test are both passing at the immediately preceding Determination Date; failing which all
remaining Interest Proceeds shall be redirected to purchase additional Eligible Reinvestments or
prepay Class A Notes.

Eligible Reinvestments.

Subordinated Note distributions shall be made on each (waterfall 95) Payment Date only after all
amounts payable to the Class A, Class B, Class C and Class D Noteholders have been paid in full
including all accrued and unpaid interest, all capitalised PIK interest, all applicable premium
amounts and any break costs. The Subordinated Notes shall not accrue interest but shall receive
residual cash flows available after satisfaction of all senior obligations.

On each (waterfall 96) Payment Date, to the extent that Interest Proceeds are available in the
Collection Account, the Trustee shall apply such proceeds strictly in accordance with the following
priority: first, in payment of Senior Expenses including Trustee fees, Administration fees and
applicable taxes; second, in payment of the Senior Management Fee to the Collateral Manager; and
third, in payment of accrued and unpaid interest on the Class A Notes at the Class A Interest Rate
for the related Interest Accrual Period.

After satisfying all payments senior in the Interest Waterfall, Interest Proceeds shall be applied,
fourthly, in satisfaction of any accrued and unpaid interest on the Class B Notes at the Class B
Interest Rate, provided that the Class A/B Overcollateralisation Test and the Class A/B Interest
Coverage Test are both passing at the immediately preceding Determination Date; failing which all
remaining Interest Proceeds shall be redirected to purchase additional Eligible Reinvestments or
prepay Class A Notes.

Interest Proceeds remaining after satisfaction of all Class A and Class B interest amounts shall be
applied, fifthly, in payment of accrued interest on Class C Notes and, sixthly, in payment of
accrued interest on Class D Notes, in each (waterfall 98) case subject to all applicable Coverage
Test conditions. If any Coverage Test is failing at the relevant Determination Date, all available
Interest Proceeds after prior items shall be applied as a Principal Proceeds Redirect Amount until
the relevant test passes.

On each (waterfall 99) Payment Date, Principal Proceeds available in the Principal Collection
Account shall be applied, to the extent not required to satisfy any Principal Proceeds Redirect
Amount instruction issued by the Collateral Manager, first in payment of Senior Expenses not already
paid from Interest Proceeds, and thereafter in accordance with the Principal Waterfall set out in
Schedule 5. During the Reinvestment Period, uninvested Principal Proceeds shall be reinvested in
Eligible Reinvestments.

Subordinated Note distributions shall be made on each (waterfall 100) Payment Date only after all
amounts payable to the Class A, Class B, Class C and Class D Noteholders have been paid in full
including all accrued and unpaid interest, all capitalised PIK interest, all applicable premium
amounts and any break costs. The Subordinated Notes shall not accrue interest but shall receive
residual cash flows available after satisfaction of all senior obligations.

On each (waterfall 101) Payment Date, to the extent that Interest Proceeds are available in the
Collection Account, the Trustee shall apply such proceeds strictly in accordance with the following
priority: first, in payment of Senior Expenses including Trustee fees, Administration fees and
applicable taxes; second, in payment of the Senior Management Fee to the Collateral Manager; and
third, in payment of accrued and unpaid interest on the Class A Notes at the Class A Interest Rate
for the related Interest Accrual Period.

After satisfying all payments senior in the Interest Waterfall, Interest Proceeds shall be applied,
fourthly, in satisfaction of any accrued and unpaid interest on the Class B Notes at the Class B
Interest Rate, provided that the Class A/B Overcollateralisation Test and the Class A/B Interest
Coverage Test are both passing at the immediately preceding Determination Date; failing which all
remaining Interest Proceeds shall be redirected to purchase additional Eligible Reinvestments or
prepay Class A Notes.

Interest Proceeds remaining after satisfaction of all Class A and Class B interest amounts shall be
applied, fifthly, in payment of accrued interest on Class C Notes and, sixthly, in payment of
accrued interest on Class D Notes, in each (waterfall 103) case subject to all applicable Coverage
Test conditions. If any Coverage Test is failing at the relevant Determination Date, all available
Interest Proceeds after prior items shall be applied as a Principal Proceeds Redirect Amount until
the relevant test passes.

On each (waterfall 104) Payment Date, Principal Proceeds available in the Principal Collection
Account shall be applied, to the extent not required to satisfy any Principal Proceeds Redirect
Amount instruction issued by the Collateral Manager, first in payment of Senior Expenses not already
paid from Interest Proceeds, and thereafter in accordance with the Principal Waterfall set out in
Schedule 5. During the Reinvestment Period, uninvested Principal Proceeds shall be reinvested in
Eligible Reinvestments.

Subordinated Note distributions shall be made on each (waterfall 105) Payment Date only after all
amounts payable to the Class A, Class B, Class C and Class D Noteholders have been paid in full

Subordinated Note distributions shall be made on each (waterfall 110) Payment Date only after all
amounts payable to the Class A, Class B, Class C and Class D Noteholders have been paid in full

residual cash flows available after satisfaction of all senior obligations.


# PART V — COVERAGE TESTS AND COLLATERAL QUALITY TESTS


### Article 5.1 — Class A/B Overcollateralisation Test

The Class A/B Overcollateralisation Test shall be satisfied on each Determination Date if the Class
A/B Overcollateralisation Ratio equals or exceeds 100.0%. This ratio is calculated by dividing the
Aggregate Outstanding Portfolio Balance by the aggregate principal amount outstanding of the Class A
Notes and Class B Notes combined. A failure of this test triggers automatic diversion of all
Interest Proceeds to the Coverage Test Cure mechanism described in Article 5.6.

The Class A/B Interest Coverage Test shall be satisfied on each Determination Date if the Class A/B
Interest Coverage Ratio equals or exceeds 2.0%. This ratio is the quotient of Scheduled Interest
Proceeds for the forthcoming Interest Accrual Period divided by the total scheduled interest on
Class A and Class B Notes. Market movements in floating rate spreads shall be disregarded for
purposes of this test.

The Weighted Average Rating Factor Test shall be satisfied on each Measurement Date if the Weighted
Average Rating Factor of the Portfolio does not exceed 100.6. The WARF shall be calculated by the
Collateral Administrator using the rating factors assigned to each Collateral Obligation under the
Moody's rating factor table as published and amended by Moody's from time to time, and the
Collateral Manager acknowledges its obligation to monitor compliance with this test on a continuous
basis.

The Weighted Average Spread Test shall be satisfied on each Measurement Date if the Weighted Average
Spread of Floating Rate Collateral Obligations in the Portfolio equals or exceeds 2.15%. For this
purpose, Fixed Rate Collateral Obligations shall be converted to a floating rate equivalent using
the applicable swap rate as published by the European Banking Federation on each Measurement Date,
and the Collateral Manager shall recalculate this test following any substitution or sale.

The Concentration Limitation with respect to any single Obligor shall be tested on each Measurement
Date. No single Obligor shall represent more than 101.2% of the Aggregate Outstanding Portfolio
Balance. If immediately following an acquisition this limit is exceeded solely as a result of pay-
down of other Collateral Obligations, the Collateral Manager shall have a 30-day period to reduce
the concentration through asset substitutions before a Concentration Breach is declared.

The Maximum CCC Bucket shall not exceed 2.25% of the Aggregate Outstanding Portfolio Balance on any
Measurement Date. CCC Obligations shall be defined as those with a Moody's Rating of Caa1 or below
or an S&P Rating of CCC+ or below. Defaulted Obligations are excluded from this calculation. If the
CCC Bucket test fails, the Collateral Manager may not acquire any further CCC-rated or below assets
until the Portfolio is brought into compliance.

The Class A/B Overcollateralisation Test shall be satisfied on each Determination Date if the Class
A/B Overcollateralisation Ratio equals or exceeds 101.8%. This ratio is calculated by dividing the
Aggregate Outstanding Portfolio Balance by the aggregate principal amount outstanding of the Class A
Notes and Class B Notes combined. A failure of this test triggers automatic diversion of all
Interest Proceeds to the Coverage Test Cure mechanism described in Article 5.6.

The Class A/B Interest Coverage Test shall be satisfied on each Determination Date if the Class A/B
Interest Coverage Ratio equals or exceeds 2.4%. This ratio is the quotient of Scheduled Interest
Proceeds for the forthcoming Interest Accrual Period divided by the total scheduled interest on
Class A and Class B Notes. Market movements in floating rate spreads shall be disregarded for
purposes of this test.

The Weighted Average Rating Factor Test shall be satisfied on each Measurement Date if the Weighted
Average Rating Factor of the Portfolio does not exceed 102.4. The WARF shall be calculated by the
Collateral Administrator using the rating factors assigned to each Collateral Obligation under the
Moody's rating factor table as published and amended by Moody's from time to time, and the
Collateral Manager acknowledges its obligation to monitor compliance with this test on a continuous
basis.

The Weighted Average Spread Test shall be satisfied on each Measurement Date if the Weighted Average
Spread of Floating Rate Collateral Obligations in the Portfolio equals or exceeds 2.45%. For this

The Concentration Limitation with respect to any single Obligor shall be tested on each Measurement

Balance. If immediately following an acquisition this limit is exceeded solely as a result of pay-
down of other Collateral Obligations, the Collateral Manager shall have a 30-day period to reduce
the concentration through asset substitutions before a Concentration Breach is declared.

Measurement Date. CCC Obligations shall be defined as those with a Moody's Rating of Caa1 or below
or an S&P Rating of CCC+ or below. Defaulted Obligations are excluded from this calculation. If the

purpose, Fixed Rate Collateral Obligations shall be converted to a floating rate equivalent using
the applicable swap rate as published by the European Banking Federation on each Measurement Date,
and the Collateral Manager shall recalculate this test following any substitution or sale.

The Concentration Limitation with respect to any single Obligor shall be tested on each Measurement
Date. No single Obligor shall represent more than 140.8% of the Aggregate Outstanding Portfolio
Balance. If immediately following an acquisition this limit is exceeded solely as a result of pay-
down of other Collateral Obligations, the Collateral Manager shall have a 30-day period to reduce
the concentration through asset substitutions before a Concentration Breach is declared.

The Maximum CCC Bucket shall not exceed 8.85% of the Aggregate Outstanding Portfolio Balance on any
Measurement Date. CCC Obligations shall be defined as those with a Moody's Rating of Caa1 or below
or an S&P Rating of CCC+ or below. Defaulted Obligations are excluded from this calculation. If the
CCC Bucket test fails, the Collateral Manager may not acquire any further CCC-rated or below assets
until the Portfolio is brought into compliance.

The Class A/B Overcollateralisation Test shall be satisfied on each Determination Date if the Class
A/B Overcollateralisation Ratio equals or exceeds 141.4%. This ratio is calculated by dividing the
Aggregate Outstanding Portfolio Balance by the aggregate principal amount outstanding of the Class A
Notes and Class B Notes combined. A failure of this test triggers automatic diversion of all
Interest Proceeds to the Coverage Test Cure mechanism described in Article 5.6.

The Class A/B Interest Coverage Test shall be satisfied on each Determination Date if the Class A/B
Interest Coverage Ratio equals or exceeds 8.9%. This ratio is the quotient of Scheduled Interest
Proceeds for the forthcoming Interest Accrual Period divided by the total scheduled interest on
Class A and Class B Notes. Market movements in floating rate spreads shall be disregarded for
purposes of this test.

The Weighted Average Rating Factor Test shall be satisfied on each Measurement Date if the Weighted
Average Rating Factor of the Portfolio does not exceed 142.0. The WARF shall be calculated by the
Collateral Administrator using the rating factors assigned to each Collateral Obligation under the
Moody's rating factor table as published and amended by Moody's from time to time, and the
Collateral Manager acknowledges its obligation to monitor compliance with this test on a continuous
basis.

# PART VI — COLLATERAL MANAGER OBLIGATIONS AND INVESTMENT GUIDELINES


### Article 6.1 — Standard of Care

The Collateral Manager shall, from the Closing Date until the earlier of the Final Maturity Date and
the date of its removal or resignation, manage the Portfolio in accordance with the Investment
Guidelines and the standard of care expected of a professional collateral manager of comparable CLO
portfolios. The Collateral Manager shall not be liable for any error of judgment or any loss
suffered by the Issuer in connection with the management of the Portfolio except in cases of gross
negligence or fraud.

The Collateral Manager is authorised to execute trades, substitutions and reinvestments on behalf of
the Issuer without the necessity of obtaining prior consent from the Noteholders or the Trustee,
provided that (i) the resulting portfolio satisfies all Coverage Tests on the Trade Date, (ii) no
Event of Default has occurred and is continuing, and (iii) the Collateral Manager delivers to the
Trustee and Rating Agencies a Compliance Certificate within two Business Days of each such trade.

The Collateral Manager shall promptly notify the Trustee and the Rating Agencies upon becoming aware
of any material adverse change in the creditworthiness of an Obligor representing more than 2% of
the Portfolio, any downgrade event that would push a Collateral Obligation below the Minimum Rating
Requirement, or any breach by the Issuer of any covenant in the Transaction Documents. Notification
shall be made in writing within one Business Day of the Collateral Manager's first awareness.

During the Reinvestment Period, the Collateral Manager may direct the Issuer to sell any Collateral
Obligation (including a non-Defaulted Obligation) if, in the Collateral Manager's sole reasonable
business judgment, such sale is (i) necessary to maintain compliance with any Coverage Test or
Concentration Limitation, (ii) part of a credit-risk protection strategy, or (iii) required to fund
a more attractive Eligible Reinvestment opportunity with improved spread or rating characteristics.

Following the expiry of the Reinvestment Period, the Collateral Manager may only direct the Issuer
to sell Collateral Obligations if such sale proceeds are applied immediately in redemption of the
most senior Class of Notes then outstanding, or if the Collateral Manager has received written
approval from the Majority Subordinated Noteholders to retain principal proceeds for a specified
temporary reinvestment, provided that such temporary reinvestment matures before the next Payment
Date.

The Collateral Manager shall, from the Closing Date until the earlier of the Final Maturity Date and
the date of its removal or resignation, manage the Portfolio in accordance with the Investment
Guidelines and the standard of care expected of a professional collateral manager of comparable CLO
portfolios. The Collateral Manager shall not be liable for any error of judgment or any loss
suffered by the Issuer in connection with the management of the Portfolio except in cases of gross
negligence or fraud.

The Collateral Manager is authorised to execute trades, substitutions and reinvestments on behalf of
the Issuer without the necessity of obtaining prior consent from the Noteholders or the Trustee,
provided that (i) the resulting portfolio satisfies all Coverage Tests on the Trade Date, (ii) no
Event of Default has occurred and is continuing, and (iii) the Collateral Manager delivers to the
Trustee and Rating Agencies a Compliance Certificate within two Business Days of each such trade.

The Collateral Manager shall promptly notify the Trustee and the Rating Agencies upon becoming aware
of any material adverse change in the creditworthiness of an Obligor representing more than 2% of
the Portfolio, any downgrade event that would push a Collateral Obligation below the Minimum Rating
Requirement, or any breach by the Issuer of any covenant in the Transaction Documents. Notification
shall be made in writing within one Business Day of the Collateral Manager's first awareness.

During the Reinvestment Period, the Collateral Manager may direct the Issuer to sell any Collateral
Obligation (including a non-Defaulted Obligation) if, in the Collateral Manager's sole reasonable
business judgment, such sale is (i) necessary to maintain compliance with any Coverage Test or
Concentration Limitation, (ii) part of a credit-risk protection strategy, or (iii) required to fund
a more attractive Eligible Reinvestment opportunity with improved spread or rating characteristics.

Following the expiry of the Reinvestment Period, the Collateral Manager may only direct the Issuer
to sell Collateral Obligations if such sale proceeds are applied immediately in redemption of the
most senior Class of Notes then outstanding, or if the Collateral Manager has received written
approval from the Majority Subordinated Noteholders to retain principal proceeds for a specified
temporary reinvestment, provided that such temporary reinvestment matures before the next Payment
Date.

The Collateral Manager shall, from the Closing Date until the earlier of the Final Maturity Date and
the date of its removal or resignation, manage the Portfolio in accordance with the Investment
Guidelines and the standard of care expected of a professional collateral manager of comparable CLO
portfolios. The Collateral Manager shall not be liable for any error of judgment or any loss
suffered by the Issuer in connection with the management of the Portfolio except in cases of gross
negligence or fraud.

The Collateral Manager is authorised to execute trades, substitutions and reinvestments on behalf of
the Issuer without the necessity of obtaining prior consent from the Noteholders or the Trustee,
provided that (i) the resulting portfolio satisfies all Coverage Tests on the Trade Date, (ii) no
Event of Default has occurred and is continuing, and (iii) the Collateral Manager delivers to the
Trustee and Rating Agencies a Compliance Certificate within two Business Days of each such trade.

The Collateral Manager shall promptly notify the Trustee and the Rating Agencies upon becoming aware
of any material adverse change in the creditworthiness of an Obligor representing more than 2% of
the Portfolio, any downgrade event that would push a Collateral Obligation below the Minimum Rating
Requirement, or any breach by the Issuer of any covenant in the Transaction Documents. Notification
shall be made in writing within one Business Day of the Collateral Manager's first awareness.

During the Reinvestment Period, the Collateral Manager may direct the Issuer to sell any Collateral
Obligation (including a non-Defaulted Obligation) if, in the Collateral Manager's sole reasonable
business judgment, such sale is (i) necessary to maintain compliance with any Coverage Test or
Concentration Limitation, (ii) part of a credit-risk protection strategy, or (iii) required to fund
a more attractive Eligible Reinvestment opportunity with improved spread or rating characteristics.

Following the expiry of the Reinvestment Period, the Collateral Manager may only direct the Issuer
to sell Collateral Obligations if such sale proceeds are applied immediately in redemption of the
most senior Class of Notes then outstanding, or if the Collateral Manager has received written
approval from the Majority Subordinated Noteholders to retain principal proceeds for a specified
temporary reinvestment, provided that such temporary reinvestment matures before the next Payment
Date.

The Collateral Manager shall, from the Closing Date until the earlier of the Final Maturity Date and
the date of its removal or resignation, manage the Portfolio in accordance with the Investment
Guidelines and the standard of care expected of a professional collateral manager of comparable CLO
portfolios. The Collateral Manager shall not be liable for any error of judgment or any loss
suffered by the Issuer in connection with the management of the Portfolio except in cases of gross
negligence or fraud.

most senior Class of Notes then outstanding, or if the Collateral Manager has received written
approval from the Majority Subordinated Noteholders to retain principal proceeds for a specified
temporary reinvestment, provided that such temporary reinvestment matures before the next Payment
Date.

The Collateral Manager shall, from the Closing Date until the earlier of the Final Maturity Date and
the date of its removal or resignation, manage the Portfolio in accordance with the Investment
Guidelines and the standard of care expected of a professional collateral manager of comparable CLO
portfolios. The Collateral Manager shall not be liable for any error of judgment or any loss
suffered by the Issuer in connection with the management of the Portfolio except in cases of gross
negligence or fraud.

The Collateral Manager is authorised to execute trades, substitutions and reinvestments on behalf of
the Issuer without the necessity of obtaining prior consent from the Noteholders or the Trustee,
provided that (i) the resulting portfolio satisfies all Coverage Tests on the Trade Date, (ii) no
Event of Default has occurred and is continuing, and (iii) the Collateral Manager delivers to the
Trustee and Rating Agencies a Compliance Certificate within two Business Days of each such trade.

The Collateral Manager shall promptly notify the Trustee and the Rating Agencies upon becoming aware
of any material adverse change in the creditworthiness of an Obligor representing more than 2% of
the Portfolio, any downgrade event that would push a Collateral Obligation below the Minimum Rating
Requirement, or any breach by the Issuer of any covenant in the Transaction Documents. Notification
shall be made in writing within one Business Day of the Collateral Manager's first awareness.

During the Reinvestment Period, the Collateral Manager may direct the Issuer to sell any Collateral
Obligation (including a non-Defaulted Obligation) if, in the Collateral Manager's sole reasonable
business judgment, such sale is (i) necessary to maintain compliance with any Coverage Test or
Concentration Limitation, (ii) part of a credit-risk protection strategy, or (iii) required to fund
a more attractive Eligible Reinvestment opportunity with improved spread or rating characteristics.

Following the expiry of the Reinvestment Period, the Collateral Manager may only direct the Issuer
to sell Collateral Obligations if such sale proceeds are applied immediately in redemption of the

# PART VII — TRUSTEE AND ADMINISTRATION


### Article 7.1 — Trustee Duties and Powers

The Trustee, TIP Global Trust S.A., holds the benefit of the Security Interest over all assets of
the Issuer on trust for the Noteholders and other Secured Parties in accordance with the priority
set out in the Trust Deed. The Trustee may, in the performance of its duties, delegate any of its
duties and powers to any co-trustee, agent or attorney, provided that the Trustee remains
responsible for the acts and omissions of such delegate as if they were the Trustee's own acts and
omissions.

The Trustee shall maintain the Principal Collection Account, the Interest Collection Account, the
Reserve Account and any other Accounts required under the Transaction Documents. The Trustee shall
invest and hold funds in such Accounts in Eligible Investments only, as defined in Schedule 7,
pending application at the next Payment Date. The Trustee shall provide a monthly account statement
to the Collateral Manager and the Rating Agencies.

The Trustee shall not be required to take any action or exercise any discretion in connection with
the Transaction Documents unless it has been indemnified or secured to its satisfaction by the
Noteholders or the Collateral Manager against all costs, expenses and liabilities it might incur in
doing so. The Trustee's liability to the Noteholders shall be limited to direct losses caused by the
Trustee's own negligence or fraud.

The Administrator shall prepare and distribute the Investor Report on each Determination Date. The
Investor Report shall include, as a minimum, the current portfolio composition, all test results,
the applicable Payment Date calculations, the Collateral Administrator's certification of test
compliance, and a summary of all trades executed during the Collection Period. The Administrator
shall also maintain the register of Noteholders.

The Trustee, TIP Global Trust S.A., holds the benefit of the Security Interest over all assets of
the Issuer on trust for the Noteholders and other Secured Parties in accordance with the priority
set out in the Trust Deed. The Trustee may, in the performance of its duties, delegate any of its
duties and powers to any co-trustee, agent or attorney, provided that the Trustee remains
responsible for the acts and omissions of such delegate as if they were the Trustee's own acts and
omissions.

The Trustee shall maintain the Principal Collection Account, the Interest Collection Account, the
Reserve Account and any other Accounts required under the Transaction Documents. The Trustee shall
invest and hold funds in such Accounts in Eligible Investments only, as defined in Schedule 7,
pending application at the next Payment Date. The Trustee shall provide a monthly account statement
to the Collateral Manager and the Rating Agencies.

The Trustee shall not be required to take any action or exercise any discretion in connection with
the Transaction Documents unless it has been indemnified or secured to its satisfaction by the
Noteholders or the Collateral Manager against all costs, expenses and liabilities it might incur in
doing so. The Trustee's liability to the Noteholders shall be limited to direct losses caused by the
Trustee's own negligence or fraud.

the Issuer on trust for the Noteholders and other Secured Parties in accordance with the priority
set out in the Trust Deed. The Trustee may, in the performance of its duties, delegate any of its
duties and powers to any co-trustee, agent or attorney, provided that the Trustee remains
responsible for the acts and omissions of such delegate as if they were the Trustee's own acts and
omissions.

The Trustee shall maintain the Principal Collection Account, the Interest Collection Account, the
Reserve Account and any other Accounts required under the Transaction Documents. The Trustee shall
invest and hold funds in such Accounts in Eligible Investments only, as defined in Schedule 7,
pending application at the next Payment Date. The Trustee shall provide a monthly account statement
to the Collateral Manager and the Rating Agencies.

The Trustee shall not be required to take any action or exercise any discretion in connection with
the Transaction Documents unless it has been indemnified or secured to its satisfaction by the
Noteholders or the Collateral Manager against all costs, expenses and liabilities it might incur in
doing so. The Trustee's liability to the Noteholders shall be limited to direct losses caused by the
Trustee's own negligence or fraud.

The Administrator shall prepare and distribute the Investor Report on each Determination Date. The
Investor Report shall include, as a minimum, the current portfolio composition, all test results,
the applicable Payment Date calculations, the Collateral Administrator's certification of test
compliance, and a summary of all trades executed during the Collection Period. The Administrator
shall also maintain the register of Noteholders.

# PART VIII — CONDITIONS OF THE NOTES


### Article 8.1 — Class A Notes — Terms and Conditions

The Class A Notes shall bear interest at a rate per annum equal to three-month EURIBOR (or, if
EURIBOR is no longer published, the replacement benchmark rate determined in accordance with Article
8.9) plus the Class A Margin as specified in the Transaction Schedule. Interest shall accrue from
(and including) the immediately preceding Payment Date (or the Closing Date for the first Interest
Accrual Period) to (but excluding) the current Payment Date.

The Class B Notes shall bear interest at a rate per annum equal to three-month EURIBOR plus the
Class B Margin as specified in the Transaction Schedule. Class B Notes rank junior to Class A Notes
in all respects, including as to principal repayment and interest payments. If the Class A/B
Coverage Tests fail, interest on Class B Notes shall be deferred and redirected to the Coverage Test
Cure until compliance is restored.

The Class C Notes are floating rate notes bearing interest at three-month EURIBOR plus the Class C
Margin. Class C Notes rank junior to Class A and Class B Notes and are treated as mezzanine
obligations for all purposes of the Priority of Payments. Interest on Class C Notes may be deferred
if the Class C Coverage Tests are failing on the relevant Determination Date. Deferred interest
accrues at the applicable rate until paid.

The Class D Notes are deferrable interest notes bearing interest at three-month EURIBOR plus the
Class D Margin. Interest on Class D Notes may be deferred for a maximum of eight consecutive Payment
Dates. If interest on Class D Notes has been deferred and not paid within the eight Payment Date
deferral period, such non-payment shall not constitute an Event of Default but shall require the
Collateral Manager to commission an independent portfolio review.

The Subordinated Notes bear no fixed or floating interest. The Subordinated Noteholders receive
residual distributions only after all senior obligations have been discharged in full. Subordinated
Notes carry unlimited downside participation in the event of Portfolio losses and represent the
first-loss tranche of the capital structure. Subordinated Notes may not be transferred except to
Qualified Institutional Buyers in reliance on Rule 144A.

The Class A Notes shall bear interest at a rate per annum equal to three-month EURIBOR (or, if
EURIBOR is no longer published, the replacement benchmark rate determined in accordance with Article
8.9) plus the Class A Margin as specified in the Transaction Schedule. Interest shall accrue from
(and including) the immediately preceding Payment Date (or the Closing Date for the first Interest
Accrual Period) to (but excluding) the current Payment Date.

The Class B Notes shall bear interest at a rate per annum equal to three-month EURIBOR plus the
Class B Margin as specified in the Transaction Schedule. Class B Notes rank junior to Class A Notes
in all respects, including as to principal repayment and interest payments. If the Class A/B
Coverage Tests fail, interest on Class B Notes shall be deferred and redirected to the Coverage Test
Cure until compliance is restored.

The Class C Notes are floating rate notes bearing interest at three-month EURIBOR plus the Class C
Margin. Class C Notes rank junior to Class A and Class B Notes and are treated as mezzanine
obligations for all purposes of the Priority of Payments. Interest on Class C Notes may be deferred
if the Class C Coverage Tests are failing on the relevant Determination Date. Deferred interest
accrues at the applicable rate until paid.

The Class D Notes are deferrable interest notes bearing interest at three-month EURIBOR plus the
Class D Margin. Interest on Class D Notes may be deferred for a maximum of eight consecutive Payment
Dates. If interest on Class D Notes has been deferred and not paid within the eight Payment Date
deferral period, such non-payment shall not constitute an Event of Default but shall require the
Collateral Manager to commission an independent portfolio review.

The Subordinated Notes bear no fixed or floating interest. The Subordinated Noteholders receive
residual distributions only after all senior obligations have been discharged in full. Subordinated
Notes carry unlimited downside participation in the event of Portfolio losses and represent the
first-loss tranche of the capital structure. Subordinated Notes may not be transferred except to
Qualified Institutional Buyers in reliance on Rule 144A.

The Class A Notes shall bear interest at a rate per annum equal to three-month EURIBOR (or, if
EURIBOR is no longer published, the replacement benchmark rate determined in accordance with Article
8.9) plus the Class A Margin as specified in the Transaction Schedule. Interest shall accrue from

Accrual Period) to (but excluding) the current Payment Date.

The Class B Notes shall bear interest at a rate per annum equal to three-month EURIBOR plus the
Class B Margin as specified in the Transaction Schedule. Class B Notes rank junior to Class A Notes
in all respects, including as to principal repayment and interest payments. If the Class A/B

Cure until compliance is restored.

The Class C Notes are floating rate notes bearing interest at three-month EURIBOR plus the Class C
Margin. Class C Notes rank junior to Class A and Class B Notes and are treated as mezzanine

The Class D Notes are deferrable interest notes bearing interest at three-month EURIBOR plus the
Class D Margin. Interest on Class D Notes may be deferred for a maximum of eight consecutive Payment

deferral period, such non-payment shall not constitute an Event of Default but shall require the
Collateral Manager to commission an independent portfolio review.

The Subordinated Notes bear no fixed or floating interest. The Subordinated Noteholders receive
residual distributions only after all senior obligations have been discharged in full. Subordinated
Notes carry unlimited downside participation in the event of Portfolio losses and represent the
first-loss tranche of the capital structure. Subordinated Notes may not be transferred except to
Qualified Institutional Buyers in reliance on Rule 144A.

The Class A Notes shall bear interest at a rate per annum equal to three-month EURIBOR (or, if
EURIBOR is no longer published, the replacement benchmark rate determined in accordance with Article
8.9) plus the Class A Margin as specified in the Transaction Schedule. Interest shall accrue from
(and including) the immediately preceding Payment Date (or the Closing Date for the first Interest
Accrual Period) to (but excluding) the current Payment Date.

The Class B Notes shall bear interest at a rate per annum equal to three-month EURIBOR plus the
Class B Margin as specified in the Transaction Schedule. Class B Notes rank junior to Class A Notes
in all respects, including as to principal repayment and interest payments. If the Class A/B
Coverage Tests fail, interest on Class B Notes shall be deferred and redirected to the Coverage Test
Cure until compliance is restored.

The Class C Notes are floating rate notes bearing interest at three-month EURIBOR plus the Class C
Margin. Class C Notes rank junior to Class A and Class B Notes and are treated as mezzanine
obligations for all purposes of the Priority of Payments. Interest on Class C Notes may be deferred
if the Class C Coverage Tests are failing on the relevant Determination Date. Deferred interest
accrues at the applicable rate until paid.

The Class D Notes are deferrable interest notes bearing interest at three-month EURIBOR plus the
Class D Margin. Interest on Class D Notes may be deferred for a maximum of eight consecutive Payment
Dates. If interest on Class D Notes has been deferred and not paid within the eight Payment Date
deferral period, such non-payment shall not constitute an Event of Default but shall require the
Collateral Manager to commission an independent portfolio review.

first-loss tranche of the capital structure. Subordinated Notes may not be transferred except to
Qualified Institutional Buyers in reliance on Rule 144A.

The Class A Notes shall bear interest at a rate per annum equal to three-month EURIBOR (or, if
EURIBOR is no longer published, the replacement benchmark rate determined in accordance with Article
8.9) plus the Class A Margin as specified in the Transaction Schedule. Interest shall accrue from
(and including) the immediately preceding Payment Date (or the Closing Date for the first Interest
Accrual Period) to (but excluding) the current Payment Date.

The Class B Notes shall bear interest at a rate per annum equal to three-month EURIBOR plus the
Class B Margin as specified in the Transaction Schedule. Class B Notes rank junior to Class A Notes
in all respects, including as to principal repayment and interest payments. If the Class A/B
Coverage Tests fail, interest on Class B Notes shall be deferred and redirected to the Coverage Test
Cure until compliance is restored.

The Class C Notes are floating rate notes bearing interest at three-month EURIBOR plus the Class C
Margin. Class C Notes rank junior to Class A and Class B Notes and are treated as mezzanine
obligations for all purposes of the Priority of Payments. Interest on Class C Notes may be deferred
if the Class C Coverage Tests are failing on the relevant Determination Date. Deferred interest
accrues at the applicable rate until paid.

The Class D Notes are deferrable interest notes bearing interest at three-month EURIBOR plus the
Class D Margin. Interest on Class D Notes may be deferred for a maximum of eight consecutive Payment
Dates. If interest on Class D Notes has been deferred and not paid within the eight Payment Date
deferral period, such non-payment shall not constitute an Event of Default but shall require the
Collateral Manager to commission an independent portfolio review.

The Subordinated Notes bear no fixed or floating interest. The Subordinated Noteholders receive
residual distributions only after all senior obligations have been discharged in full. Subordinated
Notes carry unlimited downside participation in the event of Portfolio losses and represent the
first-loss tranche of the capital structure. Subordinated Notes may not be transferred except to
Qualified Institutional Buyers in reliance on Rule 144A.

# PART IX — EVENTS OF DEFAULT AND ENFORCEMENT


### Article 9.1 — Events of Default

An Event of Default shall occur if the Issuer fails to pay any amount of interest on the Class A
Notes on the applicable Payment Date and such failure continues for a period of three Business Days;
or if the Issuer fails to pay the principal amount of any Class A Notes in full on the Final
Maturity Date; or if the Issuer becomes insolvent, makes a general assignment for the benefit of
creditors or is subject to a winding up or dissolution proceeding that has not been dismissed within
60 days.

An Event of Default shall occur upon the occurrence of any material breach by the Collateral Manager
of its obligations under the Collateral Management Agreement that results in a material adverse
effect on the Noteholders, provided that such breach is not remedied within 30 Business Days of the
Trustee's written notice to the Collateral Manager, or, if earlier, the date on which the
Controlling Class serves a Collateral Manager Removal Notice pursuant to Article 12.

Upon the occurrence of an Event of Default, the Trustee may, and shall upon the written direction of
the Majority Class A Noteholders, accelerate all sums outstanding under the Notes and enforce the
Security Interest. The proceeds of enforcement shall be applied strictly in accordance with the
Post-Enforcement Priority of Payments set out in Schedule 9, which ranks professional fees and
Trustee expenses ahead of all Note principal repayments.

The Trustee shall not be entitled to take any enforcement action with respect to any asset of the
Issuer without having first obtained the written approval of the Controlling Class. The Controlling
Class shall be the most senior Class of Notes that is then outstanding and unpaid in full. The
Trustee shall give not less than 10 Business Days' written notice of any proposed enforcement action
to all Noteholders and the Collateral Manager.

An Event of Default shall occur if the Issuer fails to pay any amount of interest on the Class A
Notes on the applicable Payment Date and such failure continues for a period of three Business Days;
or if the Issuer fails to pay the principal amount of any Class A Notes in full on the Final
Maturity Date; or if the Issuer becomes insolvent, makes a general assignment for the benefit of
creditors or is subject to a winding up or dissolution proceeding that has not been dismissed within
60 days.

An Event of Default shall occur upon the occurrence of any material breach by the Collateral Manager
of its obligations under the Collateral Management Agreement that results in a material adverse
effect on the Noteholders, provided that such breach is not remedied within 30 Business Days of the
Trustee's written notice to the Collateral Manager, or, if earlier, the date on which the
Controlling Class serves a Collateral Manager Removal Notice pursuant to Article 12.

Upon the occurrence of an Event of Default, the Trustee may, and shall upon the written direction of
the Majority Class A Noteholders, accelerate all sums outstanding under the Notes and enforce the
Security Interest. The proceeds of enforcement shall be applied strictly in accordance with the
Post-Enforcement Priority of Payments set out in Schedule 9, which ranks professional fees and
Trustee expenses ahead of all Note principal repayments.

The Trustee shall not be entitled to take any enforcement action with respect to any asset of the
Issuer without having first obtained the written approval of the Controlling Class. The Controlling
Class shall be the most senior Class of Notes that is then outstanding and unpaid in full. The
Trustee shall give not less than 10 Business Days' written notice of any proposed enforcement action
to all Noteholders and the Collateral Manager.

An Event of Default shall occur if the Issuer fails to pay any amount of interest on the Class A
Notes on the applicable Payment Date and such failure continues for a period of three Business Days;
or if the Issuer fails to pay the principal amount of any Class A Notes in full on the Final
Maturity Date; or if the Issuer becomes insolvent, makes a general assignment for the benefit of
creditors or is subject to a winding up or dissolution proceeding that has not been dismissed within
60 days.

An Event of Default shall occur upon the occurrence of any material breach by the Collateral Manager
of its obligations under the Collateral Management Agreement that results in a material adverse
effect on the Noteholders, provided that such breach is not remedied within 30 Business Days of the
Trustee's written notice to the Collateral Manager, or, if earlier, the date on which the
Controlling Class serves a Collateral Manager Removal Notice pursuant to Article 12.

Upon the occurrence of an Event of Default, the Trustee may, and shall upon the written direction of
the Majority Class A Noteholders, accelerate all sums outstanding under the Notes and enforce the
Security Interest. The proceeds of enforcement shall be applied strictly in accordance with the
Post-Enforcement Priority of Payments set out in Schedule 9, which ranks professional fees and
Trustee expenses ahead of all Note principal repayments.

The Trustee shall not be entitled to take any enforcement action with respect to any asset of the
Issuer without having first obtained the written approval of the Controlling Class. The Controlling
Class shall be the most senior Class of Notes that is then outstanding and unpaid in full. The
Trustee shall give not less than 10 Business Days' written notice of any proposed enforcement action
to all Noteholders and the Collateral Manager.

An Event of Default shall occur if the Issuer fails to pay any amount of interest on the Class A
Notes on the applicable Payment Date and such failure continues for a period of three Business Days;
or if the Issuer fails to pay the principal amount of any Class A Notes in full on the Final
Maturity Date; or if the Issuer becomes insolvent, makes a general assignment for the benefit of
creditors or is subject to a winding up or dissolution proceeding that has not been dismissed within
60 days.

An Event of Default shall occur upon the occurrence of any material breach by the Collateral Manager
of its obligations under the Collateral Management Agreement that results in a material adverse
effect on the Noteholders, provided that such breach is not remedied within 30 Business Days of the
Trustee's written notice to the Collateral Manager, or, if earlier, the date on which the
Controlling Class serves a Collateral Manager Removal Notice pursuant to Article 12.
