Class shall be the most senior Class of Notes that is then outstanding and unpaid in full. The
Trustee shall give not less than 10 Business Days' written notice of any proposed enforcement action
to all Noteholders and the Collateral Manager.

An Event of Default shall occur if the Issuer fails to pay any amount of interest on the Class A
Notes on the applicable Payment Date and such failure continues for a period of three Business Days;
or if the Issuer fails to pay the principal amount of any Class A Notes in full on the Final
Maturity Date; or if the Issuer becomes insolvent, makes a general assignment for the benefit of
creditors or is subject to a winding up or dissolution proceeding that has not been dismissed within
60 days.

An Event of Default shall occur upon the occurrence of any material breach by the Collateral Manager
of its obligations under the Collateral Management Agreement that results in a material adverse
effect on the Noteholders, provided that such breach is not remedied within 30 Business Days of the
Trustee's written notice to the Collateral Manager, or, if earlier, the date on which the
Controlling Class serves a Collateral Manager Removal Notice pursuant to Article 12.

Upon the occurrence of an Event of Default, the Trustee may, and shall upon the written direction of
the Majority Class A Noteholders, accelerate all sums outstanding under the Notes and enforce the
Security Interest. The proceeds of enforcement shall be applied strictly in accordance with the
Post-Enforcement Priority of Payments set out in Schedule 9, which ranks professional fees and
Trustee expenses ahead of all Note principal repayments.

The Trustee shall not be entitled to take any enforcement action with respect to any asset of the
Issuer without having first obtained the written approval of the Controlling Class. The Controlling
Class shall be the most senior Class of Notes that is then outstanding and unpaid in full. The
Trustee shall give not less than 10 Business Days' written notice of any proposed enforcement action
to all Noteholders and the Collateral Manager.

Notes on the applicable Payment Date and such failure continues for a period of three Business Days;
or if the Issuer fails to pay the principal amount of any Class A Notes in full on the Final
Maturity Date; or if the Issuer becomes insolvent, makes a general assignment for the benefit of
creditors or is subject to a winding up or dissolution proceeding that has not been dismissed within
60 days.

effect on the Noteholders, provided that such breach is not remedied within 30 Business Days of the
Trustee's written notice to the Collateral Manager, or, if earlier, the date on which the

Upon the occurrence of an Event of Default, the Trustee may, and shall upon the written direction of

# PART X — AMENDMENTS AND WAIVERS


### Article 10.1 — Amendment Procedure

The Transaction Documents may be amended with the written consent of the Issuer, the Trustee and the
Majority Noteholders of each Class of Notes materially adversely affected by such amendment. No
amendment shall reduce the interest rate applicable to any Class of Notes or extend the Final
Maturity Date without the prior written consent of all Noteholders of each affected Class, the
Collateral Manager and each Rating Agency.

The Investment Guidelines may be amended by the Collateral Manager with the prior written consent of
the Trustee and the Controlling Class. Any amendment to the Investment Guidelines that would
materially change the nature or risk profile of the Portfolio shall require Rating Agency
confirmation that such amendment shall not result in a downgrade or withdrawal of the rating of any
Class of Notes.

The Collateral Manager may from time to time, with prior written notice to the Trustee, amend the
Concentration Limitations in Schedule 3 to expand permitted concentrations for specific Industry or
Country categories; provided that no such amendment shall cause any Class of Notes to fail any
applicable Coverage Test or cause the Portfolio to fail the Weighted Average Spread Test on the date
the amendment becomes effective.

The Transaction Documents may be amended with the written consent of the Issuer, the Trustee and the
Majority Noteholders of each Class of Notes materially adversely affected by such amendment. No
amendment shall reduce the interest rate applicable to any Class of Notes or extend the Final
Maturity Date without the prior written consent of all Noteholders of each affected Class, the
Collateral Manager and each Rating Agency.

The Transaction Documents may be amended with the written consent of the Issuer, the Trustee and the
Majority Noteholders of each Class of Notes materially adversely affected by such amendment. No
amendment shall reduce the interest rate applicable to any Class of Notes or extend the Final
Maturity Date without the prior written consent of all Noteholders of each affected Class, the
Collateral Manager and each Rating Agency.

The Investment Guidelines may be amended by the Collateral Manager with the prior written consent of
the Trustee and the Controlling Class. Any amendment to the Investment Guidelines that would
materially change the nature or risk profile of the Portfolio shall require Rating Agency
confirmation that such amendment shall not result in a downgrade or withdrawal of the rating of any
Class of Notes.

The Collateral Manager may from time to time, with prior written notice to the Trustee, amend the
Concentration Limitations in Schedule 3 to expand permitted concentrations for specific Industry or
Country categories; provided that no such amendment shall cause any Class of Notes to fail any
applicable Coverage Test or cause the Portfolio to fail the Weighted Average Spread Test on the date
the amendment becomes effective.

# PART XV — RISK FACTORS


### Article 15.1 — Credit Risk Factors

Investment in the Notes involves a significant degree of risk. The Notes are complex financial
instruments and may not be a suitable investment for all investors. Prospective investors should
ensure that they fully understand the nature of the Notes, the extent of their exposure to risk, and
that the Notes are consistent with their investment objectives, financial situation and risk
tolerance. Past performance of the Portfolio is not a reliable indicator of future results.

The Collateral Obligations in the Portfolio are leveraged loans extended to borrowers with
substantial indebtedness relative to cash flow. Such borrowers are more susceptible to economic
downturns and adverse business developments. In a deteriorating economic environment, default rates
among highly leveraged borrowers may increase materially, and recovery values on defaulted loans may
be significantly lower than currently anticipated.

The Portfolio is subject to market risk arising from fluctuations in credit spreads, interest rates,
currency exchange rates and general market liquidity conditions. Mark-to-market losses in the
Portfolio could impair the Coverage Tests and result in a diversion of cash flows away from the
junior Classes of Notes. Market risk is exacerbated during periods of financial market stress when
correlations between Obligor defaults increase.

Prepayment rates on Collateral Obligations are inherently uncertain and may differ materially from
projections. Higher-than-expected prepayments may result in the Collateral Manager being unable to
reinvest Principal Proceeds in instruments with equivalent spread, thereby increasing the weighted
average life of the Notes. Lower-than-expected prepayments may extend the life of the Portfolio
beyond projected timelines.

The Notes are subject to liquidity risk. There is no established secondary market for the Notes, and
investors may not be able to sell their Notes at prices reflecting their fundamental value,
particularly during periods of market dislocation. Each holder of Notes should be prepared to hold
their Notes to maturity. The lack of a liquid secondary market may exacerbate mark-to-market losses
during periods of market stress.

The transaction is exposed to model risk arising from the use of rating agency models to assess the
credit quality of the Portfolio. Rating agency models are based on historical data and may not
accurately predict future default rates. Material changes in rating agency methodologies could cause
ratings of the Collateral Obligations or the Notes to be downgraded, potentially triggering Coverage
Test failures.

There can be no assurance that the Collateral Manager will have adequate opportunities to reinvest
Principal Proceeds in Eligible Reinvestments during the Reinvestment Period. Market conditions may
limit the availability of senior secured loans meeting the Eligibility Criteria at spreads
sufficient to maintain the Weighted Average Spread Test. The Issuer's exposure to reinvestment risk
increases when CLO deal issuance volumes are high and the loan market is competitive.

Currency risk arises from Collateral Obligations denominated in currencies other than Euro. Although
currency risk is substantially hedged through the Hedging Agreement, basis risk may remain between
the hedged and unhedged portions of the Portfolio. In the event that the Hedge Counterparty
defaults, the Issuer may be exposed to significant adverse currency movements until a replacement
hedge is arranged.

instruments and may not be a suitable investment for all investors. Prospective investors should
ensure that they fully understand the nature of the Notes, the extent of their exposure to risk, and

the hedged and unhedged portions of the Portfolio. In the event that the Hedge Counterparty
defaults, the Issuer may be exposed to significant adverse currency movements until a replacement
hedge is arranged.

Investment in the Notes involves a significant degree of risk. The Notes are complex financial
instruments and may not be a suitable investment for all investors. Prospective investors should
ensure that they fully understand the nature of the Notes, the extent of their exposure to risk, and
that the Notes are consistent with their investment objectives, financial situation and risk
tolerance. Past performance of the Portfolio is not a reliable indicator of future results.

The Collateral Obligations in the Portfolio are leveraged loans extended to borrowers with
substantial indebtedness relative to cash flow. Such borrowers are more susceptible to economic
downturns and adverse business developments. In a deteriorating economic environment, default rates
among highly leveraged borrowers may increase materially, and recovery values on defaulted loans may
be significantly lower than currently anticipated.

The Portfolio is subject to market risk arising from fluctuations in credit spreads, interest rates,
currency exchange rates and general market liquidity conditions. Mark-to-market losses in the
Portfolio could impair the Coverage Tests and result in a diversion of cash flows away from the
junior Classes of Notes. Market risk is exacerbated during periods of financial market stress when
correlations between Obligor defaults increase.

Prepayment rates on Collateral Obligations are inherently uncertain and may differ materially from
projections. Higher-than-expected prepayments may result in the Collateral Manager being unable to
reinvest Principal Proceeds in instruments with equivalent spread, thereby increasing the weighted
average life of the Notes. Lower-than-expected prepayments may extend the life of the Portfolio
beyond projected timelines.

The Notes are subject to liquidity risk. There is no established secondary market for the Notes, and
investors may not be able to sell their Notes at prices reflecting their fundamental value,
particularly during periods of market dislocation. Each holder of Notes should be prepared to hold
their Notes to maturity. The lack of a liquid secondary market may exacerbate mark-to-market losses
during periods of market stress.

The transaction is exposed to model risk arising from the use of rating agency models to assess the
credit quality of the Portfolio. Rating agency models are based on historical data and may not
accurately predict future default rates. Material changes in rating agency methodologies could cause
ratings of the Collateral Obligations or the Notes to be downgraded, potentially triggering Coverage
Test failures.

There can be no assurance that the Collateral Manager will have adequate opportunities to reinvest
Principal Proceeds in Eligible Reinvestments during the Reinvestment Period. Market conditions may
limit the availability of senior secured loans meeting the Eligibility Criteria at spreads
sufficient to maintain the Weighted Average Spread Test. The Issuer's exposure to reinvestment risk
increases when CLO deal issuance volumes are high and the loan market is competitive.

instruments and may not be a suitable investment for all investors. Prospective investors should
ensure that they fully understand the nature of the Notes, the extent of their exposure to risk, and
that the Notes are consistent with their investment objectives, financial situation and risk
tolerance. Past performance of the Portfolio is not a reliable indicator of future results.

The Collateral Obligations in the Portfolio are leveraged loans extended to borrowers with
substantial indebtedness relative to cash flow. Such borrowers are more susceptible to economic
downturns and adverse business developments. In a deteriorating economic environment, default rates
among highly leveraged borrowers may increase materially, and recovery values on defaulted loans may
be significantly lower than currently anticipated.

The Portfolio is subject to market risk arising from fluctuations in credit spreads, interest rates,
currency exchange rates and general market liquidity conditions. Mark-to-market losses in the
Portfolio could impair the Coverage Tests and result in a diversion of cash flows away from the
junior Classes of Notes. Market risk is exacerbated during periods of financial market stress when
correlations between Obligor defaults increase.

Prepayment rates on Collateral Obligations are inherently uncertain and may differ materially from
projections. Higher-than-expected prepayments may result in the Collateral Manager being unable to
reinvest Principal Proceeds in instruments with equivalent spread, thereby increasing the weighted
average life of the Notes. Lower-than-expected prepayments may extend the life of the Portfolio
beyond projected timelines.

The Notes are subject to liquidity risk. There is no established secondary market for the Notes, and
investors may not be able to sell their Notes at prices reflecting their fundamental value,
particularly during periods of market dislocation. Each holder of Notes should be prepared to hold
their Notes to maturity. The lack of a liquid secondary market may exacerbate mark-to-market losses
during periods of market stress.

The transaction is exposed to model risk arising from the use of rating agency models to assess the
credit quality of the Portfolio. Rating agency models are based on historical data and may not
accurately predict future default rates. Material changes in rating agency methodologies could cause
ratings of the Collateral Obligations or the Notes to be downgraded, potentially triggering Coverage
Test failures.

There can be no assurance that the Collateral Manager will have adequate opportunities to reinvest
Principal Proceeds in Eligible Reinvestments during the Reinvestment Period. Market conditions may
limit the availability of senior secured loans meeting the Eligibility Criteria at spreads
sufficient to maintain the Weighted Average Spread Test. The Issuer's exposure to reinvestment risk
increases when CLO deal issuance volumes are high and the loan market is competitive.

Currency risk arises from Collateral Obligations denominated in currencies other than Euro. Although
currency risk is substantially hedged through the Hedging Agreement, basis risk may remain between
the hedged and unhedged portions of the Portfolio. In the event that the Hedge Counterparty
defaults, the Issuer may be exposed to significant adverse currency movements until a replacement
hedge is arranged.

Investment in the Notes involves a significant degree of risk. The Notes are complex financial
instruments and may not be a suitable investment for all investors. Prospective investors should
ensure that they fully understand the nature of the Notes, the extent of their exposure to risk, and

# PART XI — REPORTING, RECORD KEEPING AND AUDIT


### Article 11.1 — Regulatory Reporting Obligations

The Issuer shall, and shall procure that the Collateral Manager shall, submit all required
regulatory reports to the competent authorities within the timelines prescribed by applicable law.
Pursuant to the SFTR regulatory reporting regime, the Issuer shall notify its approved trade
repository of all securities financing transactions on a T+1 basis. Failure to submit such
regulatory reports on time shall constitute a material breach of this Agreement, and the Issuer
shall immediately inform the Trustee of any such filing delay or non-submission.

Pursuant to Article 26 of the EU Securitisation Regulation, the Issuer shall make available to
Noteholders and to competent authorities all transaction-level data and disclosure obligations
required under the applicable regulatory disclosure framework. The Issuer shall file the required
notification with ESMA and the relevant national competent authority within the timeframes
prescribed by the applicable Regulatory Technical Standards, and shall disclose all required
information on the ESMA securitisation repository portal.

The Issuer shall maintain complete and accurate records of all Transaction Documents, all trade
confirmations, Communications Records, account statements and Compliance Certificates for a minimum
period of seven years following the date of each transaction or communication. Such records shall be
maintained in an audit trail format that is readily accessible and retrievable by the Competent
Authority upon request. The Collateral Manager shall document all trade decisions and maintain a
trade log as evidence of compliance.

Each quarterly Investor Report shall be filed with the Trustee and retained in the official register
of the Issuer for a period of not less than ten years. All records relating to Coverage Test
calculations, documentation of Eligible Reinvestments, and journal entries reflecting the Priority
of Payments shall be maintained in a ledger that is subject to annual external audit. The Collateral
Administrator shall certify the accuracy of all such records on each Determination Date.

The Collateral Manager shall maintain a detailed log of all investment decisions, trade rationales
and risk assessments undertaken in connection with the management of the Portfolio. Such
documentation shall be retained for regulatory audit purposes for a minimum of five years and shall
be made available to ESMA, the CBI and any other competent regulatory authority upon written
request. The Collateral Manager shall ensure that all such records are stored in a regulatory-
compliant documentation system.

The Collateral Administrator shall prepare and circulate the Monthly Portfolio Summary to the
Collateral Manager and Trustee within three Business Days of the end of each calendar month. The
Monthly Portfolio Summary shall list all Collateral Obligations by name, outstanding principal
balance, current rating, spread and remaining maturity. The Collateral Manager shall review the
Monthly Portfolio Summary and notify the Administrator of any discrepancies within five Business
Days.

The Collateral Manager shall notify the Trustee of any Defaulted Obligation within one Business Day
of learning of the relevant default event. The notification shall include the name of the Obligor,
principal balance, estimated recovery value and a brief description of the default circumstances.
The Collateral Manager shall include a recommended course of action, including disposal timeline and
expected recovery proceeds.

The Collateral Administrator shall prepare and circulate the Monthly Portfolio Summary to the
Collateral Manager and Trustee within three Business Days of the end of each calendar month. The
Monthly Portfolio Summary shall list all Collateral Obligations by name, outstanding principal
balance, current rating, spread and remaining maturity. The Collateral Manager shall review the
Monthly Portfolio Summary and notify the Administrator of any discrepancies within five Business
Days.

of learning of the relevant default event. The notification shall include the name of the Obligor,
principal balance, estimated recovery value and a brief description of the default circumstances.
The Collateral Manager shall include a recommended course of action, including disposal timeline and
expected recovery proceeds.

Monthly Portfolio Summary and notify the Administrator of any discrepancies within five Business
Days.

The Collateral Manager shall notify the Trustee of any Defaulted Obligation within one Business Day
of learning of the relevant default event. The notification shall include the name of the Obligor,
principal balance, estimated recovery value and a brief description of the default circumstances.
The Collateral Manager shall include a recommended course of action, including disposal timeline and
expected recovery proceeds.

The Collateral Administrator shall prepare and circulate the Monthly Portfolio Summary to the
Collateral Manager and Trustee within three Business Days of the end of each calendar month. The
Monthly Portfolio Summary shall list all Collateral Obligations by name, outstanding principal
balance, current rating, spread and remaining maturity. The Collateral Manager shall review the
Monthly Portfolio Summary and notify the Administrator of any discrepancies within five Business
Days.

# PART XII — DATA PROTECTION AND PRIVACY COMPLIANCE


### Article 12.1 — GDPR Obligations and Data Controller Responsibilities

The Issuer, acting as Data Controller within the meaning of the General Data Protection Regulation
(GDPR), shall provide a Privacy Notice to all natural persons whose personal data is processed in
connection with the Transaction. Such Privacy Notice shall comply with GDPR Articles 13 and 14 and
shall disclose the lawful basis for processing, the categories of personal data processed, the
identity of the Data Controller, retention periods and the rights available to the data subject
under applicable data protection law.

Personal data processed in connection with the Transaction shall be retained for no longer than is
necessary for the purpose for which it was collected. Investor identification data collected for
AML/KYC purposes shall be retained for a minimum of five years following the end of the business
relationship, in accordance with the Fourth Anti-Money Laundering Directive. After the applicable
retention period expires, all personal data shall be securely deleted or anonymised in line with the
data lifecycle management policy.

The Issuer shall implement a data retention schedule that specifies the retention period for each
category of personal data processed in connection with the Transaction. The retention schedule shall
be reviewed annually by the Data Protection Officer and updated to reflect changes in applicable
law. Data subjects shall be informed of the applicable retention period as part of the Privacy
Notice provided at the time of data collection.

Where the Issuer processes personal data on the basis of the data subject's consent, such consent
shall be freely given, specific, informed and unambiguous as required by GDPR Article 7. Consent
shall be obtained through a clear affirmative action and shall not be bundled with acceptance of
contractual terms. The Issuer shall maintain a consent management log and ensure that the withdrawal
of consent can be effected by the data subject at any time without detriment.

All personal data processing activities carried out in connection with the Transaction must be based
on a valid lawful basis under GDPR Article 6. The Issuer shall identify and document the legal basis
for each processing activity in the Record of Processing Activities maintained pursuant to GDPR
Article 30. Where processing is based on legitimate interest, a legitimate interest assessment shall
be conducted and documented prior to commencing the processing activity.

The Issuer shall establish and maintain procedures to honour data subject access requests, requests
for erasure, requests for restriction of processing and data portability requests within the
timeframes prescribed by GDPR Articles 15 to 22. All requests shall be acknowledged within 72 hours.
Subject access requests shall be responded to fully within one calendar month of receipt. Where a
request is complex, this period may be extended by up to two further months with the Issuer giving
written notice of the extension.

Data subjects whose personal data is processed by the Issuer shall have the right to erasure under
GDPR Article 17, subject to any overriding legal obligation to retain such data, including
regulatory record-keeping requirements. Subject access requests shall be processed free of charge
unless the request is manifestly unfounded or excessive. The Data Protection Officer shall maintain
a SAR register documenting all erasure requests received and the actions taken in response to each.

The Issuer shall not transfer personal data to recipients located in third countries outside the
European Economic Area unless such transfer is covered by an adequacy decision of the European
Commission, an appropriate safeguard such as Standard Contractual Clauses, or a derogation under
GDPR Article 49. Any cross-border transfer of investor personal data to the transaction service
providers in the United States shall be subject to an international transfer impact assessment
conducted by the DPO.

All cross-border transfers of personal data relating to the Noteholders to sub-processors and third
parties located outside the EEA shall be governed by legally binding Standard Contractual Clauses as
approved by the European Commission. The Issuer shall conduct a transfer impact assessment prior to
any such cross-border personal data transfer and shall document the outcome of such assessment in
the ROPA maintained by the DPO.

The Issuer shall disclose in its Privacy Notice the identity of all third parties and data
processors who receive or process personal data on behalf of the Issuer. The Issuer shall enter into
a Data Processing Agreement with each processor in accordance with GDPR Article 28, which shall set
out the subject-matter, duration, nature and purpose of each processing activity. The Issuer shall
maintain an up-to-date processor register and shall conduct periodic due diligence reviews of all
data processors.

The Data Protection Officer of the Issuer has been registered with the Luxembourg CNPD as required
by GDPR Article 37 and shall act as the primary point of contact for all data protection queries
raised by Noteholders, competent authorities or data subjects. The DPO shall provide an annual data
protection compliance report to the Board of Directors and the Collateral Manager.

The Issuer shall implement and maintain appropriate technical and organisational measures to protect
personal data against unauthorised access, accidental loss or destruction, in accordance with GDPR
Article 32. Security measures shall include access controls, data encryption, network segregation
and regular penetration testing of all systems that process personal data.

The Data Protection Officer of the Issuer has been registered with the Luxembourg CNPD as required
by GDPR Article 37 and shall act as the primary point of contact for all data protection queries
raised by Noteholders, competent authorities or data subjects. The DPO shall provide an annual data
protection compliance report to the Board of Directors and the Collateral Manager.

# PART XIII — THIRD-PARTY MANAGEMENT AND OUTSOURCING


### Article 13.1 — Service Provider Due Diligence

The Issuer shall enter into a written Data Processing Agreement with each third party processor to
whom it delegates the processing of personal data in connection with the Transaction. The DPA shall
include all mandatory provisions required by GDPR Article 28(3), including the subject-matter and
duration of processing, the nature and purpose of the processing, the types of personal data
processed, and the obligations and rights of the Issuer as Data Controller. Sub-processor
engagements shall require prior written authorisation.

The Collateral Manager shall conduct due diligence on all material outsourced service providers
prior to appointment, including credit risk assessment, operational risk review and compliance
verification. All material outsourcing arrangements shall comply with the EBA Guidelines on
Outsourcing Arrangements. The Collateral Manager, as agent, shall ensure that each service provider
agreement contains appropriate data protection and confidentiality obligations.

Where the Issuer engages a sub-processor pursuant to the Data Processing Agreement, such sub-
processor engagement shall be subject to the same data protection obligations as those imposed on
the primary processor under GDPR Article 28. The Issuer shall remain fully liable for the acts and
omissions of any sub-processor engaged in the performance of the contractor's obligations. All sub-
processor engagements shall be documented in the data processor register maintained by the DPO.

The Issuer shall maintain a vendor management register documenting all key service provider
contracts, including the nature of services provided, applicable contractual terms, data protection
agreement status, disaster recovery arrangements and escalation procedures. The delegate
relationship with each key service provider shall be reviewed at least annually by the Compliance
Officer, who shall document the findings in the annual outsourcing review report submitted to the
Board.

Any sub-contractor or agent appointed by the Collateral Manager to provide CLO management support
services shall be approved in advance by the Trustee. The Collateral Manager shall remain
responsible for all acts and omissions of any such sub-contractor or agent and shall not use the
appointment of a sub-contractor to reduce the standard of care owed to the Noteholders. The
Collateral Manager shall disclose all active service provider relationships in each quarterly
Investor Report.

The Collateral Manager may dismiss and replace any administrative service provider upon 30 days'
written notice to the Trustee. Replacement service providers shall be subject to the same due
diligence and approval process as the original appointee. The Trustee shall not unreasonably
withhold consent to any replacement appointment where the incoming provider meets all applicable
regulatory and credit standards.

The Collateral Administrator shall prepare and maintain the transaction data systems through which
all Portfolio data is managed and reported. All systems used to calculate Coverage Tests and
Collateral Quality Tests shall be subject to independent validation by the external auditor
annually. The Collateral Manager shall be given access to all Portfolio data systems on a read-only
basis at all times.

The Collateral Manager may dismiss and replace any administrative service provider upon 30 days'
written notice to the Trustee. Replacement service providers shall be subject to the same due
diligence and approval process as the original appointee. The Trustee shall not unreasonably
withhold consent to any replacement appointment where the incoming provider meets all applicable
regulatory and credit standards.

The Collateral Administrator shall prepare and maintain the transaction data systems through which
all Portfolio data is managed and reported. All systems used to calculate Coverage Tests and
Collateral Quality Tests shall be subject to independent validation by the external auditor
annually. The Collateral Manager shall be given access to all Portfolio data systems on a read-only
basis at all times.

diligence and approval process as the original appointee. The Trustee shall not unreasonably
withhold consent to any replacement appointment where the incoming provider meets all applicable
regulatory and credit standards.

The Collateral Administrator shall prepare and maintain the transaction data systems through which
all Portfolio data is managed and reported. All systems used to calculate Coverage Tests and
Collateral Quality Tests shall be subject to independent validation by the external auditor
annually. The Collateral Manager shall be given access to all Portfolio data systems on a read-only
basis at all times.

The Collateral Manager may dismiss and replace any administrative service provider upon 30 days'
written notice to the Trustee. Replacement service providers shall be subject to the same due
diligence and approval process as the original appointee. The Trustee shall not unreasonably
withhold consent to any replacement appointment where the incoming provider meets all applicable
regulatory and credit standards.

# PART XIV — GENERAL AND MISCELLANEOUS PROVISIONS


### Article 14.1 — Governing Law and Jurisdiction

This Offering Circular and each Transaction Document shall be governed by and construed in
accordance with the laws of Ireland. The courts of Ireland shall have exclusive jurisdiction to
settle any dispute arising out of or in connection with this Offering Circular, except that the
Trustee may take proceedings in any other jurisdiction in connection with the enforcement of the
Security Interest in circumstances where such proceedings are required in the relevant jurisdiction.

Notices under this Offering Circular shall be delivered by hand, by registered post or by email
(with read receipt) to the addresses specified in Schedule 12. Notices delivered by hand shall be
effective on delivery. Notices sent by registered post shall be effective two Business Days after
posting. Notices sent by email shall be effective upon receipt of the sender's read receipt
confirmation.

If any provision of this Offering Circular is held to be invalid, illegal or unenforceable by a
court of competent jurisdiction, that provision shall be severed from this Offering Circular without
affecting the validity or enforceability of the remaining provisions. The parties agree to replace
any severed provision with a valid and enforceable provision that as nearly as possible achieves the
original commercial intent of the severed provision.

This Offering Circular constitutes the entire agreement between the parties in relation to the
subject matter hereof and supersedes all prior agreements, representations and warranties made by
any party in relation thereto. No variation to this Offering Circular shall be binding unless in
writing and signed by or on behalf of authorised representatives of all relevant parties.

The Issuer shall bear all costs and expenses, including legal fees, incurred in connection with the
issuance of the Notes and the execution of the Transaction Documents up to the aggregate amount
specified in the Transaction Schedule. Post-Closing costs incurred in connection with the
administration of the Transaction shall be paid as Senior Expenses from the Priority of Payments at
each Payment Date.

issuance of the Notes and the execution of the Transaction Documents up to the aggregate amount
specified in the Transaction Schedule. Post-Closing costs incurred in connection with the
administration of the Transaction shall be paid as Senior Expenses from the Priority of Payments at
each Payment Date.

This Offering Circular and each Transaction Document shall be governed by and construed in
accordance with the laws of Ireland. The courts of Ireland shall have exclusive jurisdiction to
settle any dispute arising out of or in connection with this Offering Circular, except that the
Trustee may take proceedings in any other jurisdiction in connection with the enforcement of the
Security Interest in circumstances where such proceedings are required in the relevant jurisdiction.

Notices under this Offering Circular shall be delivered by hand, by registered post or by email

effective on delivery. Notices sent by registered post shall be effective two Business Days after
posting. Notices sent by email shall be effective upon receipt of the sender's read receipt
confirmation.

# PART XVI — SCHEDULES AND SUPPLEMENTAL PROVISIONS


### Article 16.1 — Schedule 1 — Eligibility Criteria Cross-Reference Table

For the avoidance of doubt, the Eligibility Criteria shall apply to each Collateral Obligation on
both the Trade Date and the Settlement Date of the relevant acquisition unless otherwise specified
in the Investment Guidelines. Any waiver of an Eligibility Criterion by the Collateral Manager shall
require advance written consent from the Trustee and shall be noted in the next Monthly Portfolio
Summary and Investor Report.

Each rating requirement set out in this Schedule shall be interpreted by reference to the published
rating scales of Moody's Investors Service and Standard & Poor's Ratings Services. Where a
Collateral Obligation has only one public rating, that rating shall be used. Where it has no public
rating, the Collateral Manager shall assign a shadow rating using the documented internal rating
model.

The Minimum Weighted Average Recovery Rate of 37% shall be calculated as the weighted average of
Moody's Junior Rating Factor recovery rates for each Collateral Obligation in the Portfolio,
weighted by Principal Balance as a proportion of the Aggregate Outstanding Portfolio Balance.

Summary and Investor Report.

The Minimum Weighted Average Recovery Rate of 46% shall be calculated as the weighted average of
Moody's Junior Rating Factor recovery rates for each Collateral Obligation in the Portfolio,
weighted by Principal Balance as a proportion of the Aggregate Outstanding Portfolio Balance.

For the avoidance of doubt, the Eligibility Criteria shall apply to each Collateral Obligation on
both the Trade Date and the Settlement Date of the relevant acquisition unless otherwise specified
in the Investment Guidelines. Any waiver of an Eligibility Criterion by the Collateral Manager shall
require advance written consent from the Trustee and shall be noted in the next Monthly Portfolio
Summary and Investor Report.

# PART XVII — ADDITIONAL BOILERPLATE AND SIGNATURE PAGES

Each representation and warranty made by the Collateral Manager under Article 6 shall be deemed
repeated on each Trade Date and on each Measurement Date by reference to the facts and circumstances
existing at that time. The Collateral Manager shall notify the Trustee immediately upon becoming
aware of any facts that would render any representation or warranty materially incorrect.

The Transaction Documents shall be binding upon and inure to the benefit of the parties thereto and
their respective successors and permitted assigns. None of the rights or obligations of the Issuer
under any Transaction Document may be assigned or transferred without the prior written consent of
the Trustee and the Controlling Class of Noteholders.

Where any consent, approval or authorisation of the Trustee is required under this Offering
Circular, such consent shall not be unreasonably withheld or delayed. The Trustee shall respond to
any written request for consent within ten Business Days of receipt, failing which consent shall be
deemed refused.

Each representation and warranty made by the Collateral Manager under Article 6 shall be deemed
repeated on each Trade Date and on each Measurement Date by reference to the facts and circumstances
existing at that time. The Collateral Manager shall notify the Trustee immediately upon becoming
aware of any facts that would render any representation or warranty materially incorrect.

The Transaction Documents shall be binding upon and inure to the benefit of the parties thereto and
their respective successors and permitted assigns. None of the rights or obligations of the Issuer
under any Transaction Document may be assigned or transferred without the prior written consent of
the Trustee and the Controlling Class of Noteholders.

Where any consent, approval or authorisation of the Trustee is required under this Offering
Circular, such consent shall not be unreasonably withheld or delayed. The Trustee shall respond to
any written request for consent within ten Business Days of receipt, failing which consent shall be
deemed refused.

repeated on each Trade Date and on each Measurement Date by reference to the facts and circumstances

aware of any facts that would render any representation or warranty materially incorrect.
